Embedded Cost of Service Study ("ECOSS")

Exhibit___ (RDP-3)

Page 1 of 5 
 
The Brooklyn Union Gas Company d/b/a National Grid NY 
Embedded Cost of Service Study (ECOSS) Methodology 
 
INTRODUCTION 
The testimony of the Rate Design Panel describes the purpose of performing an Embedded Cost 
of Service Study (“ECOSS”), and summarizes the methodology used to prepare the ECOSS in 
this proceeding.  The testimony describes the three-step ECOSS process (functionalization, 
classification, class allocation) used to analyze each element of the revenue requirement, in order 
to assign or allocate it among the rate classes. 
This Schedule 10 describes the ECOSS methodology in more detail.  Part 1 describes the three 
ECOSS process. 
Part II describes how each element of the revenue requirement- rate base, expense and revenue- 
was assigned or allocated in each of the three steps.  Plant, expenses and revenues are identified 
using the Uniform System of Accounts (FERC Accounts).  Part II serves as a guide when 
reviewing the other schedules in this exhibit. 
Part 1. DETAILED ECOSS METHODOLOGY 
The purpose of an ECOSS is to apportion fairly a utility’s total revenue requirement, including 
plant and other investments, operating expenses, depreciation, and taxes among the rate classes 
served by the utility.  The ECOSS computes for each rate class the revenue to be collected in 
order to produce the overall required rate of return on rate base.  This information provides 
valuable guidance in revenue allocation, and in the development of rates, to recover the revenue 
requirement from all rate classes. 
The three steps of the ECOSS are: 
Functionalization-  Cost elements are separated among the utility’s basic service 
functions, by determining what physical purpose each cost element serves.  KEDNY’s 
functions are: Gas Supply and Storage; Transmission; Distribution; Metering and Billing; 
Collections; Sales Promotion; and Procurement. 
Classification-  Each functionalized cost elements is classified according as Customer-
related (connects customers to the distribution system); Demand-related (meets design 
day capacity requirements) or Commodity-related (Deliver gas commodity to customers). 
Class Allocation-  Cost elements that have been functionalized and classified are 
allocated among the service classes, using an appropriate allocation factor for each cost 
element.  Allocation factors can be external (e.g., number of customers, deliveries, design 
day requirements sales and historical bad debt experience) or internal (e.g., property 
insurance is based on plant investment). 
206


--- Page 209 ---
Exhibit___ (RDP-3) 
Schedule 10 
Page 2 of 5 
 
The Brooklyn Union Gas Company d/b/a National Grid NY 
Embedded Cost of Service Study (ECOSS) Methodology 
 
Part 2. ALLOCATOR FOR EACH ELEMENT OF THE REVENUE REQUIREMENT 
A. Rate Base 
1. Intangible Plant (FERC Accounts 301-303) 
Primarily capitalized software costs for new and updated financial and operating 
systems.  Allocated in proportion to plant investment, based on a review of the 
applications that Intangible Plant comprises. 
2. Storage Plant (FERC Accounts 360-363) 
Assets needed to meet design day and seasonal requirements, for sales customers.  
Classified as Demand and allocated based on seasonal (winter) requirements. 
3. Transmission Plant (FERC Accounts 365-368) 
Transmission plant moves natural gas from suppliers to the distribution system, and is 
sized to meet design day requirements.  Classified as Demand and allocated based on 
class design day requirements. 
4. Distribution Plant (FERC Accounts 374-386) 
Mains- Distribution mains connect customers to the distribution system (i.e., customer-
related) and provide capacity to meet design day customer requirements (i.e., demand-
related).  Therefore the cost of mains is classified as partly customer-related and partly 
demand-related.  The minimum size main was determined to be 2 inch plastic, and the 
customer component is estimated to be 37.91%, based on the minimum-system study 
performed.  The customer component was allocated among the classes based on the 
number of customers.  The demand component was allocated among the classes based 
on design-day requirements.  This allocation is discussed in detail in the testimony. 
TC Mains-  Additional costs incurred to connect Temperature Controlled (TC) 
customers were identified.  The portion representing mains extensions was directly 
assigned to the TC classes and allocated among them based on annual usage.  The 
portions of the additional costs representing services and meters were identified as part 
of the analyses for those accounts. 
Measuring & Regulating Equipment- Sized for design day operation, therefore 
classified as demand-related and allocated among the rate classes based on design-day 
requirements.  Industrial equipment is allocated among the non-residential classes. 
207


--- Page 210 ---
Exhibit___ (RDP-3) 
Schedule 10 
Page 3 of 5 
 
The Brooklyn Union Gas Company d/b/a National Grid NY 
Embedded Cost of Service Study (ECOSS) Methodology 
 
Services- Services connect the customer to the distribution mains.  There is a direct 
correspondence between number of customers and number of services1, therefore 
services are classified as customer-related.  The costs are allocated based on the current 
cost for a typical service line for each rate class. 
Meters- Meters (including installation costs and house regulators) are classified as 
customer-related, and allocated based on the current cost for a typical meter for each 
rate class. 
Land, Structures and Improvements and Other Distribution Plant- Allocated in 
proportion to all other Distribution Plant accounts. 
5. General Plant (FERC Accounts 389-399) 
Primarily Structures & improvements; Tools, shop & garage; and Office furniture and 
equipment.  General plant was functionalized, classified and class allocated in 
proportion to the direct labor content of operating and maintenance expense, because 
these assets primarily support direct labor. 
6. Depreciation Reserve (FERC Accounts 108 and 111) 
Each component was allocated in proportion to the corresponding asset cost. 
7. Other Rate Base Items 
Each component was allocated in proportion to the corresponding asset cost. 
Materials & Supplies (which includes prepayments) and Cash Working Capital (non-
supply) were allocated in proportion to non-gas O&M costs.  Accumulated Deferred 
Income Tax and CWIP were allocated in proportion to plant in service.  Regulatory 
Assets and Earnings Base Adjustment were allocated in proportion to rate base.  
Storage Gas was allocated based on seasonal (winter) requirements.  Cash Working 
Capital (supply) was allocated in proportion to gas deliveries. 
B. Expenses 
1. Gas Costs (FERC Accounts 804-813)- Allocated in proportion to GAC revenue 
2. Storage Plant O&M (FERC Accounts 840-849)- Allocated in proportion to Storage 
plant (classified as Demand and allocated based on seasonal requirements). 
                                                 
1 This is not a 1:1 correspondence; an adjustment is made for multiple-account premises. 
208


--- Page 211 ---
Exhibit___ (RDP-3) 
Schedule 10 
Page 4 of 5 
 
The Brooklyn Union Gas Company d/b/a National Grid NY 
Embedded Cost of Service Study (ECOSS) Methodology 
 
3. Transmission O&M (FERC Accounts 850-867)- Allocated in proportion to 
Transmission plant (classified as Demand and allocated based on class design day 
requirements) 
4. Distribution O&M (FERC Accounts 870-894) 
Mains, Services, Meters, Regulating Equipment- Allocated on the same basis as the 
assets to which they relate. 
Operations Supervision & Engineering and Operations Supervision & Engineering- 
Allocated based on direct labor content of O&M accounts. 
Customer Installations and Other O&M - Allocated based on a special study to identify 
the activities reflected in each account. 
5. Customer Accounts Expenses (FERC Accounts 901-919) 
These accounts were functionalized among Collections, Billing and Distribution based 
on a study of competitive functions performed by the utility.  The accounts are all 
customer-related. 
Supervision (Account 901)-  Allocated in proportion to Customer records and 
collections (Account 903). 
Meter reading (Account 902)-  Allocated based on the investment in meters. 
Customer records and collections (Account 902)-  Billing-related activities are Print 
and mail bills, Bill calculation and Billing inquiries.  Collections-related activities are a 
portion of Call center, Credit & collections, Collections and Field Collections.  
Distribution-related activities are the balance of Call center, Credit & collections, 
Collections and Field Collections, as well as Postage outbound and Postage inbound. 
Each functionalized activity was allocated among the rate classes using an appropriate 
allocation factor, and the results were summed to compute allocators for Account 903- 
Distribution, Account 903- Billing and Account 903- Collections. 
Uncollectible accounts expense (Account 904)-  Allocated based on historical write-off 
experience. 
Miscellaneous customer accounts (Account 905)-  Activities are primarily customer 
contact; allocation based on number of customers. 
Customer assistance expenses (Account 908)-  Primarily costs for energy efficiency 
programs; allocated in proportion to Energy Efficiency revenue. 
209


--- Page 212 ---
Exhibit___ (RDP-3) 
Schedule 10 
Page 5 of 5 
 
The Brooklyn Union Gas Company d/b/a National Grid NY 
Embedded Cost of Service Study (ECOSS) Methodology 
 
Information & instructional (Account 909)-  Allocated based on number of customers. 
Demonstration & selling ( (Account  912) and Advertising (Account 913)-  Allocated 
based on Gas delivery volumes. 
6. Administrative & General expenses (FERC Accounts 920-939) are grouped as follows: 
 Most A&G expenses are labor-related, and are functionalized, classified and 
allocated in proportion to direct labor content of O&M accounts. 
 Plant-related expenses are functionalized, classified and allocated in proportion 
to according to gross plant. 
 Pass-through costs (18A assessment) are allocated in proportion to the revenue. 
 Other expenses comprise Franchise requirements (Account 927), Regulatory 
commission expense (Account 928) and Institutional advertising (Account 
930), which are functionalized to Distribution, classified as Demand and 
allocated based on Rate base. 
7. Depreciation and Amortization Expense (FERC Accounts 403-407) 
Each component was allocated in proportion to the corresponding asset cost. 
8. Taxes other than on income 
Property taxes were allocated in proportion to plant, excluding services and meters.  
Payroll taxes were allocated in proportion to direct labor content.  Other taxes were 
allocated in proportion to plant. 
9. Income Taxes 
Income Taxes were allocated in proportion to Pretax income. 
C. Revenue 
Revenue was functionalized and classified based on revenue requirements and 
allocated based on forecast revenue at present rates in the Historic Test Year. 
Rent from gas property was allocated based on design day demand, reflecting the 
assets that give rise to the revenue.  Late payment revenue was allocated based on 
historical write-off experience.  Billing to ESCOs, which is on a per-customer basis, 
was allocated based on number of customers.  Other Revenues were allocated based on 
gas deliveries.  Joint Facilities revenue was allocated based on design day 
requirements.
